The D'Alembertian and the Laplacian: The Patched Articles

What this list is

Every article that prints $\Box_\Phi$ or $\Delta_\Phi$ — the two glyphs carrying the material subscript — and, for each of those articles, every glyph it prints, marked or not. This is the whole of the material chart. The companion article, The D'Alembertian and the Laplacian: A Census of Two Unmarked Glyphs, states the rule and counts every class; it holds no row for the articles below.

The rule is one sentence: a formula that prints $r'_t$ or $r_\eta$ is in the material chart, so its $\Box$ is $\Box_\Phi$ and its $\Delta$ is $\Delta_\Phi$. A formula that prints $h_t$ or $h'_\eta$ is in the informational chart, so its glyph is $\Box_\Psi$ or $\Delta_\Psi$. A formula that prints $R_\mu$, or prints no coordinate, names the operator in the algebra and takes the bare glyph or $\Delta_Q$.

134 articles, 710 $\Phi$-marked glyphs. The count is on each glyph, the formula quoted as the article writes it, and the two sections at the end give the rest of each article so that no line of it is missing here.

The Count

710 occurrences of a $\Phi$-marked glyph, in 134 articles. $\Box_\Phi$ accounts for 397 in 116 articles; $\Delta_\Phi$ for 313 in 89. 71 articles carry both glyphs, 18 carry $\Delta_\Phi$ alone and 45 carry $\Box_\Phi$ alone.

what is counted occurrences articles
$\Box_\Phi$, the material d'Alembertian 397 116
$\Delta_\Phi$, the material Laplacian 313 89
every other glyph in the same articles 1188 —
total 1898 134

What This List Does Not Say

It does not say that every Laplacian is marked. The glyphs left bare outside these articles are counted and listed in the census; a bare glyph is not an unpatched one, it is a glyph whose chart the formula does not print.

It does not say that the mark is the whole of the patch. Two other things changed with it. A difference became $\delta$, and each object that had borrowed the glyph took a letter of its own. Both are in the census, under The Renaming and The Audit.

It quotes, it does not repair. A formula is printed as its article writes it. Where the source doubles a subscript or puts a power on a primed symbol, the row shows exactly that.
